## Surveys

| Date | Resource | Environments | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026‑08‑10 | [Software Engineering for and with GUI Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09278) | web | Surveys 336 GUI-agent papers through a software engineering lens, covering architectures, evaluation, and lifecycle concerns in mobile and web settings. |
| 2026‑05‑08 | [Securing Computer-Use Agents: A Unified Architecture-Lifecycle Framework for Deployment-Grounded Reliability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07110) | web, desktop, cross-platform | Survey of computer-use agent reliability and security across browsers, desktops, and mobile apps, using architecture and lifecycle views to map failures and controls. |
| 2026‑04‑24 | [Agentic World Modeling: Foundations, Capabilities, Laws, and Beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22748) | web | Survey of agentic world models that classifies predictive capabilities and governing laws, including the digital setting of web and GUI agents. |
| 2026‑01‑18 | [Agentic Artificial Intelligence (AI): Architectures, Taxonomies, and Evaluation of Large Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.12560) | web | Surveys LLM agent architectures, operating environments and evaluation, covering web navigation and native computer use alongside other agent domains. |
| 2025‑03‑30 | [A Survey of WebAgents: Towards Next-Generation AI Agents for Web Automation with Large Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23350) | web | Surveys foundation-model web agents through their architectures, training methods and trustworthiness. It organizes research on completing browser tasks from user instructions and identifies directions for further development. |
| 2024‑11‑17 | [Generalist Virtual Agents: A Survey on Autonomous Agents Across Digital Platforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.10943) | web, desktop, android, windows | Surveys virtual agents for browsers, applications and operating systems, organizing prior work by task type, observation and action spaces, model strategies and evaluation methods. It examines screen-based interaction and keyboard, mouse and touch control, alongside transferability and long-sequence decision-making challenges. |
| 2024‑02‑15 | [A Trembling House of Cards? Mapping Adversarial Attacks against Language Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10196) | web | Presents a conceptual perception–reasoning–action framework and 12 potential attack scenarios for language agents, including hypothetical web-navigation attacks. |

## Papers

| Date | Resource | Environments | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026‑11‑04 | [MKD2World: A Typed Spatial MarkDown World Model For Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06544) | web, desktop | Introduces MKD2World, a desktop web world model that represents interface elements in Typed Spatial Markdown and predicts post-action states, supported by AtomBlock-WebUI annotations. |
| 2026‑11‑04 | [Securing Web Agents with the Compile-Then-Execute Paradigm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06039) | web | Baya compiles a web task into a program using a Trusted Website API before viewing live content, limiting how webpage prompt injections can alter agent control flow. |
| 2026‑11‑03 | [Interpretable Early Termination of Web Navigation Agents via Closed Sequential Pattern Mining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04142) | web | Applies sequential pattern mining to MiniWoB++ web-agent traces, producing reviewable failure signatures and an early-termination policy to reduce token use. |
| 2026‑11‑03 | [JHiMA-WebRL: A Joint Hierarchical Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning Framework for Long-Horizon Web Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05263) | web | Introduces JHiMA-WebRL, which jointly trains a task decomposer and GUI actor with hierarchical RL for long-horizon web tasks in WebArena. |
| 2026‑11‑03 | [Voluntary Servitude: Weaponized Safety Warning Mechanisms for Controlling AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04393) | web | Studies fabricated hazard warnings in reviews that steer agents’ choices without explicit commands, including transfer to browser agents, and tests a corroboration requirement. |
| 2026‑11‑02 | [From Local Skills to Long-Horizon Web Tasks: Progressive Skill Exploration for LLM Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03116) | web | Introduces SCOPE, a training method for long web-agent tasks that discovers local skills from rollouts, composes them into chains, and expands them using failure experience. |
| 2026‑11‑02 | [WebHarbor: Docking Real Websites for Evolving GUI Agent Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03634) | web | Introduces WebHarbor, a suite of 153 executable website environments and 3,518 tasks for evaluating and training GUI web agents on interactive workflows. |
| 2026‑10‑31 | [LADS: Lease-Aware Delivery Scheduling of Tool Results for Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00881) | web | Introduces LADS, a scheduler for returning browser-action results to web agents, using cache leases and estimated resumption delay to reduce workflow latency. |
| 2026‑10‑31 | [When 200 OK Is Not an Answer: Silent Fetch Failures in Agentic Web Content Acquisition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00884) | web | Studies silent failures in web content fetched by static and rendered-browser clients, and evaluates checks for whether HTTP 200 pages contain usable content. |
| 2026‑10‑30 | [AgentShield: Fortifying Web Applications Against AI Agents and Agentic Browsers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41181) | web | Evaluates agentic browsers against web anti-bot defenses and introduces AgentShield, a behavioral fingerprinting framework for distinguishing agents from people. |
| 2026‑10‑30 | [ANGLER: Verifier-Grounded Benchmarking with an Adaptive Attacker for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41272) | web | Introduces REEF, a website benchmark with state-based attack verification, and ANGLER, which adapts attack lures using outcomes from web-agent rollouts. |
| 2026‑10‑30 | [PatchScout: Thematic Web Data Collection via Information Foraging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00169) | web | Introduces PatchScout, a multi-agent method for collecting thematic documents from the live web by deciding when to explore a new information region. |
| 2026‑10‑29 | [AgentHeal: Evaluating Goal-Preserving Resource Recovery of Web Agents under Access Barriers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39234) | web | Introduces AgentHeal, a benchmark of whether web agents detect access barriers, find legitimate alternative sources, and preserve the user's original goal. |
| 2026‑10‑29 | [DynaRev: Benchmarking Web Agents under Dynamic Goal Revision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40133) | web | Introduces DynaRev, a benchmark of 181 web-agent tasks whose goals change during execution, with trajectory-based measures of correctness, responsiveness, and efficiency. |
| 2026‑10‑29 | [InterA11y: Interactive Web Accessibility Error Detection with LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39488) | web | Introduces InterA11y, a web accessibility evaluation system that guides LLM agents with rubrics and tools for inspecting and operating pages. |
| 2026‑10‑29 | [Mage-VL-Cowork: A Full Training Recipe to Learn Hybrid Computer Use From Evolved Harnesses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40747) | desktop, web, cross-platform | Introduces Mage-VL-Cowork, a computer-use agent trained for GUI, code, and terminal actions, with Mage-CUA-Forge generating desktop and web tasks for training. |
| 2026‑10‑29 | [VeriCUA: Verification as Agentic Exploration for Computer-Using Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40578) | desktop, web, cross-platform | Introduces VeriCUA, a cross-platform task verifier that gathers evidence through GUI or code checks to assess computer-use agent completion. |
| 2026‑10‑29 | [What Does "Now" Mean to Your Computer-Use Agent? Stale Screenshots, Page Loads, and Which Check to Run Before a Click](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39282) | web | Studies clicks made from stale screenshots on web pages and provides a benchmark generator and pre-click checks for pages that change during loading. |
| 2026‑10‑27 | [Right Click, Wrong Box: Grading GUI Grounding by What a Click Does](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36831) | web | Proposes grading GUI grounding clicks by their executed outcomes and audits box-based scores on live web applications and ScreenSpot-v2 screenshots. |
| 2026‑10‑27 | [WebAnything: From the Wild Web to Scalable Agent Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37786) | web | Introduces WebAnything, a framework that reconstructs reusable, resettable website environments from real URLs for web-agent training and evaluation. |
| 2026‑10‑27 | [When to Intervene? An Empirical Study of Errors and Self-Recovery in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36471) | web | Introduces ReInBench, annotated computer-use and web-agent error traces, and ReCoF, a method for predicting whether an agent will recover without intervention. |
| 2026‑10‑26 | [Back to Basics: A Skill-Centric Approach to Generalist Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35823) | web | Introduces MiniWeb for skill-labeled browser tasks, MiniWebGen for generating training trajectories, and MiniWebAgent for composing learned skills into web workflows. |
| 2026‑10‑26 | [Harness-Model Collaboration for Long-Horizon Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34724) | web | Introduces HaMo, a continual-learning framework for web agents that retrieves strategy cards from past workflows and updates model parameters to reuse them. |
| 2026‑10‑25 | [AgentProofBench: Benchmarking Reusable Verifiers for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34634) | web | Introduces AgentProofBench, a benchmark linking tests of reusable web-agent verification rules to subsequent execution from saved web states. |
| 2026‑10‑24 | [SkillShift-Bench: Benchmarking Skill Learning under Environment Evolution for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33528) | web | Introduces SkillShift-Bench to evaluate how web agents reuse learned skills when task context, visual perception, or execution conditions change across web systems. |
| 2026‑10‑23 | [Correct Answers, Closed Gates: SolveBind for GUI Agents at the Verification Barrier](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32223) | web | Introduces SolveBind, a GUI-agent framework that decomposes verification tasks and uses runtime evidence to guide interactions with protected websites and applications. |
| 2026‑10‑22 | [AgentVQA: A Unified Benchmark for Agentic Visual Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31494) | web | AgentVQA is an offline visual question-answering benchmark across five agent domains, including web agents and questions involving UI element location. |
| 2026‑10‑20 | [InterWebCoder: Learning Interactive Webpage Generation from Executable Tests via Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28610) | web | InterWebCoder trains models to generate interactive webpage code using recorded interactions, executable tests and repair feedback, an adjacent web interface task. |
| 2026‑10‑19 | [Agent World Model in the Wild: Computer-Use Agent Environment Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26828) | web, desktop, linux | WildAWM synthesizes resettable GUI/MCP/CLI environments and cross-interface tasks, using execution-based checks of solvability, difficulty and verifier consistency before supervised and reinforcement learning. |
| 2026‑10‑19 | [Comparing Computer Use Behavior Across Multiple Levels of Abstraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26703) | desktop, web | MAP annotates computer-use traces at linked operation, function/consequence, method and goal levels, then compares human and agent workflows through sequence, pattern and effort metrics. |
| 2026‑10‑19 | [CUA-Lite: An Open Platform for Computer Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26071) | desktop, linux, windows, web, android | CUA-Lite unifies environment interfaces, supervised data formats and per-model harnesses for evaluation, reinforcement learning and supervised fine-tuning across desktop, browser and mobile tasks. |
| 2026‑10‑19 | [Deep Search in Real-World Web Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30181) | web | OpenDS-World benchmarks multi-step research on the live open Web, requiring agents to search, interact with browsers and combine evidence across sources. |
| 2026‑10‑19 | [Step-Aligned Script Generation with Verification and Recovery for Web Automation Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25601) | web | Presents TESSARA, which turns natural-language web test intent into Playwright scripts, linking each browser interaction to selector evidence, outcomes, and repair history. |
| 2026‑10‑19 | [VeriCUA: Learning Proactive Verification for Reliable GUI Agent Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26469) | desktop, linux, web, android | VeriCUA trains a Judge to select verification checks and stopping decisions while a frozen Probe inspects the post-task GUI. Single-attempt reinforcement learning balances verdict correctness and Probe use. |

| 2026‑10‑16 | [PersonaCUABench: Benchmarking Computer-Use Agents Serving Diverse Personas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22990) | web | PersonaCUABench varies simulated user requests and clarification dialogue over fixed web tasks, comparing task completion with requirement handling and persona-agent satisfaction ratings. |

Task-Redirecting Agent Persuasion Benchmark for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.23128) | web | Introduces TRAP, which evaluates task-redirection attacks embedded in realistic web interfaces. Controlled website clones and a modular injection framework support comparisons of persuasion techniques and contextual changes across agents. |
| 2025‑12‑29 | [Nested Browser-Use Learning for Agentic Information Seeking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.23647) | web | NestBrowse organizes browser actions in a nested framework that separates interaction control from page exploration. It evaluates this design for deep information seeking beyond snippet retrieval and direct URL fetching. |
| 2025‑12‑28 | [DECEPTICON: How Dark Patterns Manipulate Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.22894) | web | DECEPTICON is an environment of 700 web navigation tasks that isolates dark patterns to measure how deceptive interface designs steer agent actions. |
| 2025‑12‑19 | [DAVE: A VLM Vision Encoder for Document Understanding and Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.17221) | web | DAVE trains a vision encoder for document images and web agents using self-supervised pretraining followed by supervised parsing and localization. It combines decoder-specific encoder merging with feature fusion and evaluates web localization and agent benchmarks alongside document tasks. |
| 2025‑12‑18 | [OS-Oracle: A Comprehensive Framework for Cross-Platform GUI Critic Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.16295) | web, desktop, android | OS-Oracle synthesizes GUI critic examples and trains a pre-action critic with supervised learning followed by consistency-preserving GRPO. It introduces OS-Critic Bench across mobile, web, and desktop settings and tests critic-assisted agents in OSWorld and AndroidWorld. |
| 2025‑12‑14 | [ceLLMate: Sandboxing Browser AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.12594) | web | ceLLMate constrains browser-agent authority through an extension that applies policies at the HTTP layer. It evaluates this sandbox against prompt-injection attacks in WASP, avoiding dependence on brittle low-level click and keystroke policies. |
| 2025‑12‑14 | [WebOperator: Action-Aware Tree Search for Autonomous Agents in Web Environment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.12692) | web | WebOperator uses tree search, action filtering, and path checks before replay to explore web tasks and backtrack while accounting for irreversible actions. |
| 2025‑12‑08 | [Privacy Practices of Browser Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.07725) | web | Presents a privacy evaluation framework for browser agents covering component weaknesses, website defenses, tracking, privacy-related prompts and personal-data leakage. The framework is applied to deployed browser-agent tools. |
| 2025‑12‑07 | [Permission Manifests for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.02371) | web | Proposes agent-permissions.json, a website manifest for declaring which interactions web agents may perform, with optional references to APIs. |
| 2025‑12‑05 | [FronTalk: Benchmarking Front-End Development as Conversational Code Generation with Multi-Modal Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04203) | web | FronTalk benchmarks multi-turn front-end code generation from text and visual feedback; a web agent explores generated sites for evaluation and in AceCoder. |
| 2025‑11‑28 | [LegalWebAgent: Empowering Access to Justice via LLM-Based Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.04105) | web | LegalWebAgent separates user-needs clarification, webpage browsing, and actions such as form completion or scheduling. A 15-task evaluation studies legal-service workflows relevant to Québec civil-law users, using HTML and screenshot observations. |
| 2025‑11‑28 | [MCP vs RAG vs NLWeb vs HTML: A Comparison of the Effectiveness and Efficiency of Different Agent Interfaces to the Web (Technical Report)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.23281) | web | Compares HTML browsing, RAG, MCP, and NLWeb interfaces for web agents performing product search, comparison, and checkout tasks in four simulated e-shops. |
| 2025‑11‑27 | [Real-Time Procedural Learning From Experience for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22074) | web | PRAXIS stores action outcomes and retrieves past state-action-result examples to guide agent decisions, with evaluation on the REAL web browsing benchmark. |
| 2025‑11‑26 | [Prune4Web: DOM Tree Pruning Programming for Web Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.21398) | web | Introduces Prune4Web, where generated scoring programs filter DOM elements according to decomposed subtasks before grounding. An annotation pipeline and two-turn training procedure coordinate the planner, programmatic filter and grounder. |
| 2025‑11‑25 | [BrowseSafe: Understanding and Preventing Prompt Injection Within AI Browser Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.20597) | web | BrowseSafe builds a benchmark of prompt injections in realistic HTML for browser agents and evaluates defenses against attacks that can affect agent actions. |
| 2025‑11‑24 | [Fara-7B: An Efficient Agentic Model for Computer Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.19663) | web | Introduces FaraGen synthetic web trajectories, a compact screenshot-based action model, and WebTailBench for underrepresented browser tasks. |
| 2025‑11‑22 | [Building Browser Agents: Architecture, Security, and Practical Solutions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.19477) | web | Reports architectural and security lessons from building a browser agent, including hybrid accessibility-tree and visual context, browser tooling and programmatic constraints. A WebGames evaluation accompanies the discussion of reliability and specialization. |
| 2025‑11‑22 | [WebSTAR: Scalable Data Synthesis for Computer Use Agents with Step-Level Filtering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.10962) | web | Introduces a pipeline that filters individual steps from noisy computer-agent rollouts and augments them with reasoning for training. WebSTAR and WebSCORE support GUI-policy supervision and a distilled process reward model, StepRM. |
| 2025‑11‑19 | [Computer-Use Agents as Judges for Generative User Interface](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.15567) | web | AUI-Gym evaluates generated websites by having computer-use agents attempt tasks whose executability is checked programmatically. A feedback dashboard condenses navigation histories into visual summaries that a coding agent can use to revise an interface. |
| 2025‑11‑17 | [WebCoach: Self-Evolving Web Agents with Cross-Session Memory Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.12997) | web | Introduces WebCoach, which condenses navigation logs into persistent episodic memory and retrieves experiences by similarity and recency. Runtime hooks selectively supply advice to browser agents, evaluated on WebVoyager without retraining the underlying models. |
| 2025‑11‑13 | [Co-EPG: A Framework for Co-Evolution of Planning and Grounding in Autonomous GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10705) | web, android | Co-EPG alternates reinforcement learning for a planner with updates to a grounding model using generated training examples. The evolving grounder provides feedback to the planner, and evaluation uses Multimodal Mind2Web and AndroidControl. |
| 2025‑11‑11 | [AgentPRM: Process Reward Models for LLM Agents via Step-Wise Promise and Progress](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08325) | web | AgentPRM scores sequential agent decisions by their promise and progress toward a goal, with web shopping and browser navigation among the motivating tasks. |
| 2025‑11‑09 | [WebVIA: A Web-based Vision-Language Agentic Framework for Interactive and Verifiable UI-to-Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.06251) | web | WebVIA combines a web UI exploration agent, interactive UI-to-Code generation, and validation of generated code's interactivity. |
| 2025‑11‑08 | [SynthAgent: Adapting Web Agents with Synthetic Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.06101) | web | Introduces SynthAgent, which generates tasks by exploring website elements and refines them when observations reveal conflicts. A later trajectory-refinement stage removes misaligned behavior before fine-tuning agents for the target websites. |
| 2025‑10‑30 | [GUI Knowledge Bench: Revealing the Knowledge Gap of VLMs in GUI Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.26098) | web, desktop, windows, macos, linux, android, ios, cross-platform | GUI Knowledge Bench tests knowledge of widgets, interactions, and procedures across six named platforms and 292 applications to assess VLMs used for GUI tasks. |
| 2025‑10‑26 | [WebATLAS: An LLM Agent with Experience-Driven Memory and Action Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.22732) | web | WebATLAS is a web agent that stores interaction outcomes and simulates candidate actions before acting, using past experience to plan navigation on unseen websites. |
| 2025‑10‑22 | [See, Think, Act: Online Shopper Behavior Simulation with VLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.19245) | web | Studies online shopper action prediction with VLM agents using webpage screenshots, HTML, and action history from OPeRA, trained with SFT and hierarchical RL rewards. |
| 2025‑10‑22 | [Surfer 2: The Next Generation of Cross-Platform Computer Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.19949) | web, desktop, android | Surfer2 combines hierarchical context management with separate planning and execution, self-verification, and recovery for visual computer use. It evaluates the framework across web, desktop, and Android tasks without task-specific fine-tuning. |
| 2025‑10‑22 | [VideoAgentTrek: Computer Use Pretraining from Unlabeled Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.19488) | desktop, web | Extracts structured GUI actions from screen-recorded tutorials using inverse dynamics, then uses the inferred trajectories for computer-use training. |
| 2025‑10‑22 | [WebGraphEval: Multi-Turn Trajectory Evaluation for Web Agents using Graph Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.19205) | web | Introduces WebGraphEval, which merges multiple agents' trajectories into a weighted graph of canonical actions. Structural analyses examine recurring behavior, inefficient paths and important decision points beyond a final success score. |
| 2025‑10‑21 | [Genesis: Evolving Attack Strategies for LLM Web Agent Red-Teaming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18314) | web | Introduces Genesis, a red-teaming framework that generates adversarial injections for web agents and revises attack strategies using responses and interaction logs. |
| 2025‑10‑20 | [Investigating the Impact of Dark Patterns on LLM-Based Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18113) | web | Presents LiteAgent for recording web-agent interactions and TrickyArena for enabling or disabling dark patterns in controlled websites. Experiments compare how individual patterns, combined patterns and visual or HTML changes affect agent decisions. |
| 2025‑10‑18 | [Branch-and-Browse: Efficient and Controllable Web Exploration with Tree-Structured Reasoning and Action Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.19838) | web | Branch-and-Browse is a web agent framework that uses tree-structured subtasks, web state replay, and page action memory to explore and complete tasks. |
| 2025‑10‑17 | [PolySkill: Learning Generalizable Skills Through Polymorphic Abstraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15863) | web | Introduces PolySkill, which separates a web-agent skill's goal from its execution so agents can learn and reuse skills across websites. |
| 2025‑10‑17 | [WEBSERV: A Full-Stack and RL-Ready Web Environment for Training Web Agents at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.16252) | web | WebServ combines copy-on-write container environments with a compact DOM-derived browser interface for training web agents. Network-aware waiting supports action execution in single-page applications, with evaluations and reinforcement-learning experiments on WebArena-Lite. |
| 2025‑10‑16 | [Agentic Entropy-Balanced Policy Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.14545) | web | Introduces Agentic Entropy-Balanced Policy Optimization (AEPO), an RL method that adjusts rollout sampling and policy updates for tool-using web agents. |
| 2025‑10‑16 | [ReUseIt: Synthesizing Reusable AI Agent Workflows for Web Automation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.14308) | web | Introduces ReUseIt, which synthesizes reusable web-agent workflows from successful and failed attempts and adds guards to detect and fix execution errors. |
| 2025‑10‑16 | [WebAggregator: Enhancing Compositional Reasoning Capabilities of Deep Research Agent Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.14438) | web | Introduces WebAggregator, a pipeline for synthesizing compositional questions from websites to train deep-research agents, plus the WebAggregatorQA testbed. |
| 2025‑10‑15 | [In-Browser LLM-Guided Fuzzing for Real-Time Prompt Injection Testing in Agentic AI Browsers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.13543) | web | Proposes an in-browser fuzzing framework that uses language-model guidance to test indirect prompt-injection vulnerabilities in agentic browsers. The work focuses on unwanted actions triggered by webpage instructions under the browser agent's user privileges. |
| 2025‑10‑14 | [HackWorld: Evaluating Computer-Use Agents on Exploiting Web Application Vulnerabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.12200) | web | Introduces HackWorld for evaluating visual computer-use agents in a capture-the-flag collection of web applications. The benchmark examines interface navigation and multistep planning during vulnerability discovery and exploitation tasks. |
| 2025‑10‑13 | [R-WoM: Retrieval-augmented World Model For Computer-use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.11892) | desktop, web | R-WoM evaluates how well world models predict nearby GUI states, align complete procedures, and recognize milestone transitions. It retrieves external tutorials to guide simulated future interactions, with experiments on subsets of OSWorld and WebArena. |
| 2025‑10‑13 | [SusBench: An Online Benchmark for Evaluating Dark Pattern Susceptibility of Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.11035) | web | Introduces SusBench, an online benchmark that inserts dark-pattern designs into consumer websites for agent evaluation. A human study checks the realism of the interventions, and agent trials compare susceptibility across pattern types. |
| 2025‑10‑13 | [WebRouter: Query-specific Router via Variational Information Bottleneck for Cost-sensitive Web Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.11221) | web | Introduces WebRouter, a cost-aware router that compresses web-agent prompts to choose among LLMs, with cost and accuracy measured on WebVoyager websites. |
| 2025‑10‑12 | [BrowserAgent: Building Web Agents with Human-Inspired Web Browsing Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10666) | web | Trains BrowserAgent to use Playwright-based scrolling, clicking and typing directly on webpages for information-seeking tasks. Supervised and rejection fine-tuning are paired with explicit memory for conclusions carried across steps. |
| 2025‑10‑11 | [ALLOY: Generating Reusable Agent Workflows from User Demonstration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10049) | web | Introduces ALLOY, which turns user demonstrations into editable, reusable workflows for LLM-based web agents carrying out preference-dependent tasks. |
| 2025‑10‑11 | [SecureWebArena: A Holistic Security Evaluation Benchmark for LVLM-based Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10073) | web | Introduces SecureWebArena with simulated interactive websites, an attack-vector taxonomy and labeled agent trajectories. Its evaluation separates failures in reasoning, behavioral traces and task outcomes across vision-language agent families. |
| 2025‑10‑10 | [WARC-Bench: Web Archive Based Benchmark for GUI Subtask Executions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09872) | web | WARC-Bench packages dynamic web pages as replayable archives and evaluates short navigation subtasks involving components such as date pickers and scrollable containers. It also studies supervised training followed by reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards on these browser interactions. |
| 2025‑10‑06 | [Understanding User Experiences of Computer Use Agents: Design Space and Opportunities for Building Agent UX Prototypes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04452) | web | Maps design considerations for computer-use agent experiences and introduces AgentUXlab, a browser-based probe for prototyping and evaluating agent UX on a website. |
| 2025‑10‑05 | [JEF-Hinter: Leveraging Offline Knowledge for Improving Web Agents Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04373) | web | Introduces JEF-Hinter, which distills successful and failed trajectories into compact episodic hints and retrieves them for the current agent state. Web benchmark experiments examine guidance from decisive steps rather than full demonstration replay. |
| 2025‑10‑04 | [Cross-Modal Content Optimization for Steering Web Agent Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.03612) | web | Introduces Cross-Modal Preference Steering (CPS), a black-box attack that edits a listing's images and text to steer VLM-based web agents' selections. |
| 2025‑10‑03 | [FocusAgent: Simple Yet Effective Ways of Trimming the Large Context of Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.03204) | web | Introduces FocusAgent, which uses a lightweight LLM to select task-relevant lines from web agents' accessibility-tree observations, reducing context size and exposure to prompt injection. |
| 2025‑10‑03 | [Learning Efficient Guardrails for Compliance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.03485) | web | Introduces PolicyGuardBench for detecting policy violations in full web-agent trajectories and their prefixes, and trains PolicyGuard as an efficient guardrail model. |
| 2025‑10‑02 | [BrowserArena: Evaluating LLM Agents on Real-World Web Navigation Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.02418) | web | Introduces BrowserArena, a live-web platform for head-to-head agent comparisons on user-submitted tasks. Step-level human annotations support targeted analysis of navigation, pop-up handling and CAPTCHA-related failures. |
| 2025‑10‑01 | [Demystifying deep search: a holistic evaluation with hint-free multi-hop questions and factorised metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05137) | web | Introduces WebDetective, a deep-search benchmark with a traceable Wikipedia sandbox, and EvidenceLoop, an evidence-tracking workflow for web-agent search and synthesis. |
| 2025‑10‑01 | [Graph2Eval: Automatic Multimodal Task Generation for Agents via Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00507) | web | Introduces Graph2Eval, which generates multimodal agent evaluation tasks from knowledge graphs, including multi-step web interactions in Graph2Eval-Bench. |
| 2025‑10‑01 | [PAL-UI: Planning with Active Look-back for Vision-Based GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00413) | web, cross-platform | PAL-UI combines observation/action summaries with selective retrieval of historical screenshots during planning. It trains mobile navigation models from step-level supervision and evaluates transfer to web navigation without additional training. |
| 2025‑10‑01 | [WAInjectBench: Benchmarking Prompt Injection Detections for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01354) | web | Introduces WAInjectBench with a threat-model taxonomy and malicious and benign text-image datasets for web-agent prompt-injection detection. It compares detector families across explicit and less visible forms of injected content. |
| 2025‑10‑01 | [WALT: Web Agents that Learn Tools](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01524) | web | WALT explores website functionality and converts demonstrated interactions into validated, reusable tools combining DOM actions, URL navigation, and agentic fallbacks. It evaluates this expanded browser action space on WebArena and VisualWebArena. |
| 2025‑09‑30 | [Ferret-UI Lite: Lessons from Building Small On-Device GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.26539) | android, web, desktop, cross-platform | Ferret-UI Lite develops a 3B GUI agent using mixed real and synthetic data, reasoning with visual tools at inference, and reinforcement learning. It evaluates grounding and navigation across mobile, web, and desktop settings, including AndroidWorld and OSWorld. |
| 2025‑09‑29 | [ReasoningBank: Scaling Agent Self-Evolving with Reasoning Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25140) | web | Distills generalizable reasoning strategies from both successful and failed agent trajectories into a persistent memory bank for test-time retrieval. |
| 2025‑09‑29 | [Retrieval-augmented GUI Agents with Generative Guidelines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24183) | web, android | RAG-GUI trains a small guidance adapter to filter and summarize retrieved web tutorials while keeping the acting agent fixed. Supervised and rejection-sampling stages prepare the adapter for inference-time assistance, tested on offline web/mobile tasks and interactive Android tasks. |
| 2025‑09‑28 | [WAREX: Web Agent Reliability Evaluation on Existing Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.03285) | web | WAREX augments existing web-agent benchmarks with reliability challenges arising from unstable systems and changing or disruptive website behavior. Evaluation on WebArena, WebVoyager and REAL examines how these disturbances affect task completion. |
| 2025‑09‑26 | [Benchmarking MLLM-based Web Understanding: Reasoning, Robustness and Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21782) | web | Evaluates multimodal LLMs on web interface reasoning, visual perturbation robustness, and safety-critical web understanding tasks. |
| 2025‑09‑26 | [Can We Stop Malicious AI? KILLBENCH: A Benchmark for External AI Kill Switch Feasibility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.13725) | web | KillBench evaluates external prompt-style signals intended to halt maliciously operating web agents, across harmful scenarios and agent configurations. |
| 2025‑09‑26 | [RISK: A Framework for GUI Agents in E-commerce Risk Management](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21982) | web | RISK combines browser-interaction training data, a benchmark, and reinforcement fine-tuning for e-commerce risk-management workflows. Its trajectories and rewards distinguish individual actions from multi-step stateful tasks involving deeply embedded web content. |
| 2025‑09‑26 | [WebGen-Agent: Enhancing Interactive Website Generation with Multi-Level Feedback and Step-Level Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.22644) | web | Introduces WebGen-Agent for website code generation, using screenshot critiques and GUI-agent tests to refine code and provide step-level training rewards. |
| 2025‑09‑25 | [Recon-Act: A Self-Evolving Multi-Agent Browser-Use System via Web Reconnaissance, Tool Generation, and Task Execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21072) | web | Introduces Recon-Act, a browser-use framework that compares failed and successful web trajectories to create reusable tools for planning and executing long-horizon website tasks. |
| 2025‑09‑17 | [Detecting Pipeline Failures through Fine-Grained Analysis of Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14382) | web | Introduces a modular framework for identifying errors at intermediate stages of a web-agent pipeline. A SeeAct and Mind2Web case study examines weaknesses that are obscured by final task-success metrics. |
| 2025‑09‑17 | [TGPO: Tree-Guided Preference Optimization for Robust Web Agent Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14172) | web | TGPO merges semantically equivalent web states into tree-structured trajectories for offline preference optimization. A process reward model scores subgoal progress, redundant behavior, and action validity, while dynamic weighting emphasizes consequential decisions. |
| 2025‑09‑16 | [ReSum: Unlocking Long-Horizon Search Intelligence via Context Summarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.13313) | web | Introduces ReSum, which compresses interaction histories during long web searches, and ReSum-GRPO, which trains agents to reason across the resulting segments. |
| 2025‑09‑15 | [PrivWeb: Unobtrusive and Content-aware Privacy Protection For Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.11939) | web | Introduces PrivWeb, which uses a local language model to anonymize interface information according to user preferences. Adaptive notifications and selective pauses give users control over sensitive information access during web-agent tasks. |
| 2025‑09‑14 | [Environmental Injection Attacks against GUI Agents in Realistic Dynamic Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.11250) | web | Introduces Chameleon, an environmental-injection evaluation framework that varies webpage content and trigger context. It combines simulated page variation with attention-based optimization and tests GUI agents across realistic websites. |
| 2025‑09‑09 | [AgentSentinel: An End-to-End and Real-Time Security Defense Framework for Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.07764) | desktop, linux, web | Introduces AgentSentinel, which intercepts sensitive computer-agent operations and audits them using task context together with system traces. BadComputerUse evaluates this enforcement approach across attack scenarios involving tool and computer control. |
| 2025‑09‑02 | [UI-TARS-2 Technical Report: Advancing GUI Agent with Multi-Turn Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.02544) | web, desktop, windows, android, cross-platform | UI-TARS-2 combines scalable trajectory generation, stabilized multi-turn reinforcement learning, and a sandbox that supports GUI, filesystem, and terminal actions. The report evaluates the resulting agent across web, desktop, Android, and game tasks and analyzes training stability. |
| 2025‑09‑01 | [Throttling Web Agents Using Reasoning Gates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.01619) | web | Introduces reasoning gates that require agents to solve generated, verifiable puzzles before receiving access to resources. The throttling approach is deployed on a custom website and MCP servers and evaluated with web agents. |
| 2025‑08‑30 | [KG-RAG: Enhancing GUI Agent Decision-Making via Knowledge Graph-Driven Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.00366) | android, web, desktop, cross-platform | KG-RAG converts UI transition graphs into a searchable vector store and uses intent-guided retrieval to construct actionable navigation paths. It introduces KG-Android-Bench and KG-Harmony-Bench and evaluates transfer from mobile apps to web and desktop tasks. |
| 2025‑08‑29 | [MMSearch-Plus: Benchmarking Provenance-Aware Search for Multimodal Browsing Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21475) | web | Introduces MMSearch-Plus, a multimodal browsing benchmark with iterative image-text search, and an agent framework that marks and crops images for targeted retrieval. |
| 2025‑08‑29 | [Morae: Proactively Pausing UI Agents for User Choices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21456) | web | Introduces Morae, a UI agent that detects decision points and pauses for user choices using interface code and screenshots. A study with blind and low-vision participants evaluates this mixed-initiative approach on real web tasks. |
| 2025‑08‑23 | [WebSight: A Vision-First Architecture for Robust Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16987) | web | WebSight uses a vision-language model trained on web-interface examples within a modular agent with planning, action, verification, and episodic memory components. It evaluates element clicking on Showdown Clicks and full browsing tasks on WebVoyager using visual observations. |
| 2025‑08‑22 | [WebMMU: A Benchmark for Multimodal Multilingual Website Understanding and Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16763) | web | Introduces WebMMU, a multilingual benchmark for website visual question answering, element grounding, code editing, and mockup-to-code generation. |
| 2025‑08‑18 | [A Functionality-Grounded Benchmark for Evaluating Web Agents in E-commerce Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15832) | web | Amazon-Bench generates functionality-specific web tasks from page content and interactive controls, covering operations beyond product search. Its evaluator checks task completion and unintended changes to account state, such as address or purchase settings. |
| 2025‑08‑18 | [WebMall -- A Multi-Shop Benchmark for Evaluating Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13024) | web | WebMall provides four simulated online shops with heterogeneous product data for comparison shopping, related-product searches, and checkout tasks. It evaluates web agents with different observation formats and memory configurations in a fixed offline environment. |
| 2025‑08‑14 | [MM-BrowseComp: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Multimodal Browsing Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13186) | web | Introduces MM-BrowseComp, a benchmark for browsing agents that must use evidence from web images and videos to answer multimodal search questions. |
| 2025‑08‑12 | [FineState-Bench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Fine-Grained State Control in GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.09241) | desktop, web, cross-platform | FineState-Bench evaluates fine-grained GUI perception and control across desktop, web, and mobile interfaces. Its Visual Diagnostic Assistant separates perception and localization contributions in controlled interaction experiments. |
| 2025‑08‑07 | [Cognitive Duality for Adaptive Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.05081) | web | Introduces CogniWeb, which switches between fast reactive processing and slower deliberative reasoning according to task complexity. WebArena experiments examine this combination of learned behaviors and online planning. |
| 2025‑08‑06 | [Beyond Pixels: Exploring DOM Downsampling for LLM-Based Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04412) | web | D2Snap reduces serialized DOM observations while preserving actionable elements and features that distinguish their uses. The study compares reduced DOM inputs with screenshot-based observations for a web agent on tasks sampled from Online-Mind2Web. |
| 2025‑08‑06 | [HarmonyGuard: Toward Safety and Utility in Web Agents via Adaptive Policy Enhancement and Dual-Objective Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04010) | web | Introduces HarmonyGuard, a multi-agent framework that updates security policies and weighs safety against task completion for agents operating on the open web. |
| 2025‑08‑06 | [VeriWeb: Verifiable Long-Chain Web Benchmark for Agentic Information-Seeking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04026) | web | Introduces VeriWeb, a benchmark of long-chain web information-seeking tasks with verifiable subtasks and a complete human-annotated trajectory for each task. |
| 2025‑08‑03 | [Web-CogReasoner: Towards Multimodal Knowledge-Induced Cognitive Reasoning for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.01858) | web | Web-CogReasoner separates factual and conceptual web knowledge from procedural reasoning, using a structured curriculum to train a multimodal agent. Web-CogDataset and Web-CogBench support study of these capabilities alongside web-navigation evaluation. |
| 2025‑07‑31 | [General Agentic Planning Through Simulative Reasoning with World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.23773) | web | Introduces SiRA, an agent architecture that uses a language-based world model to simulate action outcomes for planning in web-browser tasks. |
| 2025‑07‑30 | [Magentic-UI: Towards Human-in-the-loop Agentic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.22358) | web | Magentic-UI combines browser interaction, code execution, file operations, and MCP tools in an interface for human-agent collaboration. It studies collaborative planning and task execution, action guards, and persistent memory through benchmark tasks, simulated users, user studies, and safety assessments. |
| 2025‑07‑25 | [MMBench-GUI: Hierarchical Multi-Platform Evaluation Framework for GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.19478) | web, desktop, windows, macos, linux, ios, android, cross-platform | MMBench-GUI evaluates interface understanding, element grounding, task automation, and collaboration across six named desktop, mobile, and web platforms. It introduces the Efficiency-Quality Area metric for examining execution efficiency alongside task quality in online GUI automation. |
| 2025‑07‑20 | [Manipulating LLM Web Agents with Indirect Prompt Injection Attack via HTML Accessibility Tree](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14799) | web | Studies indirect prompt injection through HTML content consumed as accessibility trees. The evaluation tests how manipulated page observations redirect browser-agent actions on real websites. |
| 2025‑07‑18 | [WebGuard: Building a Generalizable Guardrail for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14293) | web | Introduces WebGuard, a human-annotated dataset for predicting outcomes and risk levels of state-changing website actions. It provides generalization splits and evaluates specialized guardrail models. |
| 2025‑07‑17 | [Machine-Readable Ads: Accessibility and Trust Patterns for AI Web Agents interacting with Online Advertisements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12844) | web | Compares DOM-based and pixel-based web agents on a cloned news site containing advertisements, consent dialogs and paywalls. The study examines how interface presentation affects ad interaction and consequential subscription choices. |
| 2025‑07‑15 | [Let's Think in Two Steps: Mitigating Agreement Bias in MLLMs with Self-Grounded Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.11662) | web | Studies agreement bias in multimodal agent verifiers across web navigation and computer use, and introduces SGV to assess trajectories using self-generated priors. |
| 2025‑07‑09 | [VisualTrap: A Stealthy Backdoor Attack on GUI Agents via Visual Grounding Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.06899) | web, desktop | Studies backdoors that redirect GUI visual grounding even when the task plan is correct. VisualTrap evaluates whether poisoned grounding training transfers to downstream tasks and across web, mobile, and desktop interface settings. |
| 2025‑07‑08 | [OpenAgentSafety: A Comprehensive Framework for Evaluating Real-World AI Agent Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.06134) | web, desktop | Evaluates agent safety across real browser, code, filesystem, shell, and messaging tools using rule-based and model-based assessments. |
| 2025‑07‑08 | [R-VLM: Region-Aware Vision Language Model for Precise GUI Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05673) | web, cross-platform | R-VLM localizes GUI elements through zoomed-in region proposals and an intersection-over-union-aware training objective. It evaluates the grounding model on ScreenSpot and AgentStudio and studies its effect on navigation in AITW and Mind2Web. |
| 2025‑07‑07 | [A Systematization of Security Vulnerabilities in Computer Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.05445) | web | Analyzes security boundaries in computer-use agents through a risk taxonomy and concrete adversarial case studies. It links observed failures to input provenance, interface-action binding, memory, and delegation, and proposes CUA-specific evaluation principles. |
| 2025‑07‑06 | [WebSynthesis: World-Model-Guided MCTS for Efficient WebUI-Trajectory Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04370) | web | Introduces WebSynthesis, which uses a learned web world model and tree-based planning to synthesize interaction trajectories for training web agents. |
| 2025‑07‑05 | [How to Train Your LLM Web Agent: A Statistical Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04103) | web | Studies compute allocation for web-agent post-training with teacher-generated supervised fine-tuning followed by on-policy reinforcement learning. It samples 1,370 training configurations and uses bootstrap analysis to examine hyperparameter sensitivity on WorkArena and MiniWoB++. |
| 2025‑07‑04 | [Less is More: Empowering GUI Agent with Context-Aware Simplification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.03730) | web, cross-platform | SimpAgent prunes distracting interface elements with visual masks and compresses interaction history using consistency-based guidance. The study evaluates these two forms of context reduction during GUI navigation in web and mobile environments. |
| 2025‑07‑01 | [WebArxiv: A Reproducible Benchmark for Evaluating Multimodal Web Agents on arXiv Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00938) | web | WebArxiv provides 510 static-snapshot tasks with deterministic answers for scholarly browsing, including constrained paper retrieval, content extraction, and cross-paper comparison. The study also tests dynamic retrieval of interaction history to address repetitive or incomplete agent reasoning. |
| 2025‑06‑30 | [LineRetriever: Planning-Aware Observation Reduction for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.00210) | web | LineRetriever uses a language model to select observation lines that matter for upcoming web-navigation actions. It reduces DOM or accessibility-tree context by considering the planning horizon instead of relying solely on semantic similarity. |
| 2025‑06‑30 | [ZonUI-3B: A Lightweight Vision-Language Model for Cross-Resolution GUI Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23491) | web, desktop, cross-platform | ZonUI-3B trains GUI grounding with a curated 24,000-example collection of mobile, desktop, and web screenshots. Its two-stage procedure first learns across platforms and then specializes on high-resolution images, with ablations on data diversity and redundant examples. |
| 2025‑06‑26 | [Agent-RewardBench: Towards a Unified Benchmark for Reward Modeling across Perception, Planning, and Safety in Real-World Multimodal Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21252) | web | Introduces Agent-RewardBench to evaluate step-level reward models for multimodal agents across perception, planning, and safety; web navigation is among its scenarios. |
| 2025‑06‑26 | [Mind2Web 2: Evaluating Agentic Search with Agent-as-a-Judge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21506) | web | Introduces Mind2Web 2, 130 long-horizon tasks requiring live web browsing and information synthesis, plus an Agent-as-a-Judge framework for grading answers and sources. |
| 2025‑06‑18 | [Context manipulation attacks : Web agents are susceptible to corrupted memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.17318) | web | Studies context manipulation in web agents by altering stored task representations rather than webpage prompts alone. Evaluation on Browser-use and Agent-E examines memory-directed attacks and the implications for secure context handling. |
| 2025‑06‑18 | [Embodied Web Agents: Bridging Physical-Digital Realms for Integrated Agent Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15677) | web | Introduces environments that couple 3D physical-world simulations with functional web interfaces. The accompanying benchmark evaluates tasks requiring agents to coordinate online information and physical actions across cooking, navigation and shopping scenarios. |
| 2025‑06‑17 | [AgentSynth: Scalable Task Generation for Generalist Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14205) | desktop, web | AgentSynth composes easily generated subtasks into longer computer-use tasks and trajectories with adjustable difficulty. Its evaluation uses the number of subtasks to probe how agent success changes as task complexity grows. |
| 2025‑06‑12 | [Build the web for agents, not agents for the web](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10953) | web | Proposes Agentic Web Interface (AWI), a website interface intended for agent navigation, and sets out six design principles for web-agent use. |
| 2025‑06‑12 | [Mirage-1: Augmenting and Updating GUI Agent with Hierarchical Multimodal Skills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.10387) | android, web, cross-platform | Mirage-1 organizes interaction trajectories into execution, core, and meta-skills, then uses them to guide Monte Carlo tree search during online GUI tasks. It introduces AndroidLH for long-horizon evaluation and tests the approach on Android and web-navigation benchmarks. |
| 2025‑06‑09 | [EconWebArena: Benchmarking Autonomous Agents on Economic Tasks in Realistic Web Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.08136) | web | Introduces EconWebArena, a curated benchmark of economic-data tasks requiring live website navigation, multimodal interpretation, and precise extraction. It evaluates visual grounding, planning, and interaction design within multistep workflows. |
| 2025‑06‑09 | [Thinking vs. Doing: Agents that Reason by Scaling Test-Time Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07976) | web | Test-Time Interaction scales the interaction horizon so web agents can explore, backtrack and revise plans within a rollout. Its online reinforcement-learning curriculum adjusts rollout lengths and is evaluated on WebVoyager and WebArena. |
| 2025‑06‑04 | [Go-Browse: Training Web Agents with Structured Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03533) | web | Go-Browse frames web-environment exploration as graph search so that information can be reused across data-collection episodes. | 2025‑06‑03 | [DeepShop: A Benchmark for Deep Research Shopping Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02839) | web | DeepShop evaluates shopping agents with evolving query complexity across product attributes, filters and sorting preferences. Its evaluation combines checks of these individual requirements with overall task success. |
| 2025‑06‑03 | [Surfer-H Meets Holo1: Cost-Efficient Web Agent Powered by Open Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02865) | web | Describes Surfer-H, a web agent paired with the Holo1 vision-language model family trained on web content, synthetic examples, and agent-generated data. The work introduces WebClick for web-interface localization and evaluates browser task execution on WebVoyager. |
| 2025‑06‑03 | [VPI-Bench: Visual Prompt Injection Attacks for Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.02456) | web | Introduces VPI-Bench, interactive web-platform variants containing visually embedded malicious instructions. It evaluates computer-use and browser-use agents under these threats and examines system-prompt defenses. |
| 2025‑06‑02 | [FormFactory: An Interactive Benchmarking Suite for Multimodal Form-Filling Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01520) | web | Introduces FormFactory, an interactive web-based benchmark for agents filling online forms, with varied field formats and evaluation of instruction-to-field alignment. |
| 2025‑06‑02 | [MLA-Trust: Benchmarking Trustworthiness of Multimodal LLM Agents in GUI Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01616) | web, android, cross-platform | Benchmarks truthfulness, controllability, safety, and privacy of multimodal LLM agents across interactive web and mobile GUI tasks. |
| 2025‑06‑02 | [WebChoreArena: Evaluating Web Browsing Agents on Realistic Tedious Web Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01952) | web | Introduces WebChoreArena, 532 web chores built on WebArena environments to evaluate agents’ retention of page information, calculation, and tracking across pages. |
| 2025‑05‑31 | [ResTest: Targeted Coverage of Residual Not-Covered Code Using Large Language Models for Web GUI Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00520) | web | Introduces ResTest, which uses coverage reports and a state transition graph to infer untested web functions, then directs a GUI agent to execute them. |
| 2025‑05‑31 | [RiOSWorld: Benchmarking the Risk of Multimodal Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00618) | web | Introduces RiOSWorld, a computer-use risk benchmark separating user-originated and environmental threats. | 2025‑05‑30 | [Agent-X: Evaluating Deep Multimodal Reasoning in Vision-Centric Agentic Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24876) | web | Introduces Agent-X, a benchmark of multi-step visual reasoning and tool use with step-level grading across six settings, including web browsing. |
| 2025‑05‑30 | [Open CaptchaWorld: A Comprehensive Web-based Platform for Testing and Benchmarking Multimodal LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24878) | web | Open CaptchaWorld evaluates multimodal agents on interactive browser-based CAPTCHA puzzles. | 2025‑05‑30 | [VideoCAD: A Dataset and Model for Learning Long-Horizon 3D CAD UI Interactions from Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24838) | web | Introduces synthetic CAD interaction videos, a model for learning interface actions from video, and evaluation tasks for spatial and long-horizon reasoning. |
| 2025‑05‑28 | [RedTeamCUA: Realistic Adversarial Testing of Computer-Use Agents in Hybrid Web-OS Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21936) | web, desktop | RedTeamCUA combines a virtual-machine OS environment with containerized websites for controlled prompt-injection tests spanning both interfaces. | 2025‑05‑28 | [WorkForceAgent-R1: Incentivizing Reasoning Capability in LLM-based Web Agents via Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22942) | web | Uses rule-based rewards for output structure and action correctness to train reasoning for enterprise web navigation. |
| 2025‑05‑27 | [AdInject: Real-World Black-Box Attacks on Web Agents via Advertising Delivery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21499) | web | Studies advertising delivery as an indirect-injection channel for web agents under a black-box threat model. | 2025‑05‑26 | [WebCoT: Enhancing Web Agent Reasoning by Reconstructing Chain-of-Thought in Reflection, Branching, and Rollback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20013) | web | WebCoT turns reflection, look-ahead, branching and rollback procedures into chain-of-thought training rationales. | 2025‑05‑23 | [TransBench: Breaking Barriers for Transferable Graphical User Interface Agents in Dynamic Digital Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17629) | ios, android, web, cross-platform | Introduces TransBench to evaluate GUI grounding transfer across app versions, iOS, Android, Web, and functionally distinct applications. |
| 2025‑05‑22 | [WebAgent-R1: Training Web Agents via End-to-End Multi-Turn Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16421) | web | Trains web agents through asynchronous multi-turn environment interaction, using task-success rewards to optimize complete trajectories. |
| 2025‑05‑21 | [AutoData: A Multi-Agent System for Open Web Data Collection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15859) | web | Introduces AutoData, a multi-agent system that collects web datasets from a natural-language request, and Instruct2DS, a benchmark for live collection across three domains. |
| 2025‑05‑21 | [ReGUIDE: Data Efficient GUI Grounding via Spatial Reasoning and Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15259) | web | Combines self-generated localization reasoning, spatial consistency checks, and inference-time search to improve data-efficient web grounding. |
| 2025‑05‑21 | [Web-Shepherd: Advancing PRMs for Reinforcing Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15277) | web | Web-Shepherd is a process reward model for assessing individual steps of web-navigation trajectories. | 2025‑05‑19 | [GEM: Gaussian Embedding Modeling for Out-of-Distribution Detection in GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12842) | desktop, web, cross-platform | GEM detects instructions outside a GUI agent’s capabilities by modeling distances in its input-embedding space with a Gaussian mixture. | 2025‑05‑19 | [The Hidden Dangers of Browsing AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13076) | web | Outlines an end-to-end threat model and empirical security evaluation across architectural layers of autonomous browser agents. |
| 2025‑05‑16 | [WebInject: Prompt Injection Attack to Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11717) | web | Introduces WebInject, a prompt-injection attack that optimizes rendered webpage pixels to make screenshot-based web agents take attacker-chosen actions. |
| 2025‑05‑15 | [Characterizing Unintended Consequences in Human-GUI Agent Collaboration for Web Browsing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09875) | web | Studies unintended consequences of collaborative web browsing with GUI agents through social-media analysis and interviews. | 2025‑05‑09 | [AgentVigil: Generic Black-Box Red-teaming for Indirect Prompt Injection against LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05849) | web | Introduces AgentVigil, a black-box fuzzing framework for finding indirect prompt injections against LLM agents, including attacks that redirect web navigation. |
| 2025‑05‑06 | [Deep Research Bench: Evaluating AI Web Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.06287) | web | Introduces Deep Research Bench, 89 multi-step web research tasks and a frozen collection of scraped pages for evaluating search agents and their traces over time. |

| 2025‑05‑01 | [Visual Test-time Scaling for GUI Agent Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.00684) | web | RegionFocus adds visual test-time scaling by zooming into selected screenshot regions and maintaining an image-based map of landmarks and actions. | 2025‑04‑30 | [WebThinker: Empowering Large Reasoning Models with Deep Research Capability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21776) | web | Combines iterative web search and link-following with interleaved reasoning and report drafting for deep research. | 2025‑04‑23 | [PixelWeb: The First Web GUI Dataset with Pixel-Wise Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16419) | web | Introduces PixelWeb, a webpage GUI dataset with element images, contours, and pixel masks. | 2025‑04‑23 | [WebEvolver: Enhancing Web Agent Self-Improvement with Coevolving World Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21024) | web | WebEvolver trains a co-evolving world model to predict web observations and generate additional agent-training trajectories. | 2025‑04‑22 | [WASP: Benchmarking Web Agent Security Against Prompt Injection Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.18575) | web | Introduces WASP, an end-to-end benchmark for prompt-injection security in web agents. | 2025‑04‑18 | [DoomArena: A framework for Testing AI Agents Against Evolving Security Threats](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14064) | web | Presents a modular security testing framework that plugs into BrowserGym and tool-use benchmarks to evaluate agents under configurable threat models. |
| 2025‑04‑17 | [WebLists: Extracting Structured Information From Complex Interactive Websites Using Executable LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12682) | web | Introduces WebLists, a benchmark for structured extraction from interactive websites, and BardeenAgent, which turns web-agent runs into replayable CSS-selector programs. |
| 2025‑04‑16 | [WebRollback: Enhancing Web Agents with Explicit Rollback Mechanisms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11788) | web | WebRollback gives a web agent an explicit action for returning to an earlier state in its navigation trajectory. | 2025‑04‑15 | [REAL: Benchmarking Autonomous Agents on Deterministic Simulations of Real Websites](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11543) | web | Introduces REAL, a benchmark of deterministic website replicas with 112 tasks covering information retrieval and state-changing browser actions. |
| 2025‑04‑15 | [The Obvious Invisible Threat: LLM-Powered GUI Agents' Vulnerability to Fine-Print Injections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11281) | web | Characterizes six forms of GUI-content injection and studies their effects on GUI agents and human users using adversarial webpages. | 2025‑04‑14 | [Breaking the Data Barrier -- Building GUI Agents Through Task Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10127) | web, android | Studies whether mid-training on GUI perception, multimodal reasoning, and text reasoning transfers to GUI-agent planning. | 2025‑04‑14 | [RealWebAssist: A Benchmark for Long-Horizon Web Assistance with Real-World Users](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10445) | web | RealWebAssist evaluates web assistance through sequences of instructions collected from real users across multiple websites. | 2025‑04‑13 | [AgentA/B: Automated and Scalable Web A/BTesting with Interactive LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09723) | web | Introduces AgentA/B, which uses persona-based LLM agents to navigate real webpages and perform shopping interactions for simulated web A/B tests. |
| 2025‑04‑12 | [Explorer: Robust Collection of Interactable GUI Elements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09352) | android, web | Explorer collects interactable GUI elements from a live target application and records user sessions for application-specific model training. | 2025‑04‑11 | [AgentRewardBench: Evaluating Automatic Evaluations of Web Agent Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.08942) | web | AgentRewardBench evaluates automated web-agent judges against expert reviews of 1,302 trajectories from five benchmarks. | 2025‑04‑09 | [Inducing Programmatic Skills for Agentic Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06821) | web | Introduces agent skill induction (ASI), which creates, checks, and reuses program-based skills for web tasks by combining primitive actions into higher-level routines. |

| 2025‑04‑07 | [On the Robustness of GUI Grounding Models Against Image Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04716) | web, desktop, cross-platform | Evaluates GUI grounding models under natural image noise, untargeted adversarial perturbations, and targeted image attacks. | 2025‑04‑02 | [An Illusion of Progress? Assessing the Current State of Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01382) | web | Introduces Online-Mind2Web, with 300 tasks across 136 live websites, and examines how benchmark design affects assessments of web agents. It also develops an LLM judge for task completion and compares its judgments with human evaluation. |
| 2025‑04‑02 | [Are Autonomous Web Agents Good Testers?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.01495) | web | Adapts web agents to execute natural-language test steps, verify assertions, and issue test verdicts. | 2025‑03‑26 | [ShieldAgent: Shielding Agents via Verifiable Safety Policy Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.22738) | web | Proposes a guardrail agent that reasons over explicit safety policies and checks action trajectories, with a web-agent safety benchmark. |
| 2025‑03‑26 | [sudo rm -rf agentic_security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20279) | desktop, web | Evaluates security failures of computer-use agents under adversarial requests and screen-context manipulation. The study examines refusal behavior while agents perform tasks in desktop and web environments. |
| 2025‑03‑18 | [WebNav: An Intelligent Agent for Voice-Controlled Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.13843) | web | Introduces WebNav, a web navigation agent that turns natural-language commands into GUI actions using screenshot context, DOM labels, and separate LLMs for planning and action parameters. |
| 2025‑03‑12 | [AgentDAM: Privacy Leakage Evaluation for Autonomous Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09780) | web | Evaluates whether autonomous web agents minimize use of sensitive personal information when completing tasks that expose unnecessary private data. |
| 2025‑03‑12 | [Plan-and-Act: Improving Planning of Agents for Long-Horizon Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09572) | web | Separates planning from execution and creates synthetic plan annotations for training agents on extended web-navigation tasks. |
| 2025‑03‑10 | [BEARCUBS: A benchmark for computer-using web agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.07919) | web | BEARCUBS provides 111 live-web information-seeking questions with short answers and human-validated browsing trajectories. | 2025‑03‑06 | [SafeArena: Evaluating the Safety of Autonomous Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04957) | web | Pairs benign and harmful web tasks to assess deliberate misuse risks and categorize agent behavior by risk level. |
| 2025‑03‑04 | [ATLaS: Agent Tuning via Learning Critical Steps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02197) | web | Selects critical steps from expert interaction trajectories for targeted agent fine-tuning, studying efficiency and generalization across tasks. |
| 2025‑03‑04 | [Language Models can Self-Improve at State-Value Estimation for Better Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02878) | web | Introduces Self-Taught Lookahead (STL), which trains a value model to predict actions and resulting states for more efficient search, with evaluation on web-agent tasks. |
| 2025‑03‑04 | [LiteWebAgent: The Open-Source Suite for VLM-Based Web-Agent Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02950) | web | Introduces a modular visual web-agent stack with planning and memory components, exposed through a remote-browser application and Chrome extension. |
| 2025‑03‑04 | [MPO: Boosting LLM Agents with Meta Plan Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02682) | web | Refines reusable high-level meta plans with execution feedback to guide language-agent planning across interactive environments. |
| 2025‑02‑27 | [Why Are Web AI Agents More Vulnerable Than Standalone LLMs? | 2025‑02‑25 | [Towards Sustainable Web Agents: A Plea for Transparency and Dedicated Metrics for Energy Consumption](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17903) | web | Examines energy and carbon-accounting limitations in web-agent evaluation and argues for transparent, dedicated sustainability measurements. |
| 2025‑02‑25 | [WebGames: Challenging General-Purpose Web-Browsing AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18356) | web | Introduces WebGames, a browser-agent benchmark with 50+ interactive challenges in a self-contained test environment and verifiable solutions. |
| 2025‑02‑24 | [Towards Enterprise-Ready Computer Using Generalist Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01861) | web | Describes the CUGA enterprise-agent development process, emphasizing iterative evaluation, failure analysis, and refinement on software-task benchmarks. |

| 2025‑02‑18 | [UXAgent: An LLM Agent-Based Usability Testing Framework for Web Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12561) | web | Introduces UXAgent, which uses simulated users and a browser connector to test website usability and present their actions, responses, and recordings to UX researchers. |
| 2025‑02‑17 | [Explorer: Scaling Exploration-driven Web Trajectory Synthesis for Multimodal Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11357) | web | Synthesizes multimodal web trajectories through exploration and refinement, then trains a browser agent to study the effect of scaling interaction data. |
| 2025‑02‑12 | [WorldGUI: An Interactive Benchmark for Desktop GUI Automation from Any Starting Point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.08047) | desktop, web | Introduces desktop and web tasks with varied initial states to test planning recovery and adaptability beyond canonical starting configurations. |
