| 2025‑02‑11 | [Symbiotic Cooperation for Web Agents: Harnessing Complementary Strengths of Large and Small LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07942) | web | Couples large- and small-model web-agent exploration with trajectory synthesis and distillation to improve both model types iteratively. |
| 2025‑02‑10 | [InSTA: Towards Internet-Scale Training For Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06776) | web | InSTA builds a pipeline that creates website tasks, collects web-agent trajectories and filters them by judged success to train smaller web navigation agents. |
| 2025‑02‑06 | [RWKV-UI: UI Understanding with Enhanced Perception and Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.03971) | web | RWKV-UI is a visual language model for high-resolution web interfaces that uses layout detection and reasoning-chain visual prompts to understand pages. |
| 2025‑01‑31 | [Towards Computer-Using Personal Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15515) | web, cross-platform | Proposes computer-using personal agents connected to user-controlled data repositories, discussing privacy, interoperability, and collaborative tasks. |
| 2025‑01‑28 | [CowPilot: A Framework for Autonomous and Human-Agent Collaborative Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.16609) | web | Introduces CowPilot, a web-navigation framework where agents suggest actions and users can pause, reject, override, or resume them. It evaluates autonomous and collaborative use through task success and human effort. |
| 2025‑01‑21 | [R2D2: Remembering, Replaying and Dynamic Decision Making with a Reflective Agentic Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12485) | web | R2D2 gives web agents a replay-buffer map of visited pages and a reflection step for analyzing errors and revising navigation strategies. |
| 2025‑01‑20 | [Agent-R: Training Language Model Agents to Reflect via Iterative Self-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.11425) | web | Builds self-critique training examples from searched recovery paths to improve agents' ability to detect and correct errors during interaction. |
| 2025‑01‑18 | [Learn-by-interact: A Data-Centric Framework for Self-Adaptive Agents in Realistic Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.10893) | web, desktop, cross-platform | Learn-by-interact synthesizes agent interaction trajectories from documentation and derives instructions from their histories for training or in-context use across web and desktop tasks. |
| 2025‑01‑13 | [WebWalker: Benchmarking LLMs in Web Traversal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.07572) | web | Introduces WebWalkerQA and an exploration-and-critique framework for gathering information by traversing multiple levels of a website. |
| 2024‑12‑18 | [TheAgentCompany: Benchmarking LLM Agents on Consequential Real World Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14161) | web, desktop | Simulates a software-company workplace to evaluate agents on professional tasks involving websites, programs, files, and coworker communication. |
| 2024‑12‑17 | [Proposer-Agent-Evaluator(PAE): Autonomous Skill Discovery For Foundation Model Internet Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13194) | web | Combines autonomous task proposal, web interaction, and visual success evaluation to discover skills and improve browser-agent policies. |
| 2024‑12‑14 | [WEPO: Web Element Preference Optimization for LLM-based Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10742) | web | Uses web-element preference optimization with sampled negative HTML elements to align high-level user intent with navigation actions. |
| 2024‑12‑13 | [Iris: Breaking GUI Complexity with Adaptive Focus and Self-Refining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10342) | web, desktop | Combines information-sensitive image cropping with a learning loop between element description and grounding for complex interface understanding. |
| 2024‑12‑12 | [AgentTrek: Agent Trajectory Synthesis via Guiding Replay with Web Tutorials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.09605) | web | Converts public tutorials into task specifications and executes them with an agent to synthesize multimodal interaction trajectories. |
| 2024‑12‑12 | [Falcon-UI: Understanding GUI Before Following User Instructions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.09362) | web, android, cross-platform | Pretrains GUI-context understanding with instruction-free web interactions before fine-tuning Falcon-UI on instructed GUI tasks. |
| 2024‑12‑06 | [The BrowserGym Ecosystem for Web Agent Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05467) | web | Describes BrowserGym's shared web interaction environment and AgentLab tooling for consistent experiments across multiple agent benchmarks. |
| 2024‑12‑02 | [Ponder & Press: Advancing Visual GUI Agent towards General Computer Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01268) | cross-platform, web, desktop | Separates interpretation of user instructions from precise GUI localization using general-purpose and specialized visual-language components. |
| 2024‑11‑22 | [ScribeAgent: Towards Specialized Web Agents Using Production-Scale Workflow Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.15004) | web | Studies fine-tuning open language models on production web-workflow traces, including training choices for specialized web agents. |
| 2024‑11‑20 | [AdaptAgent: Adapting Multimodal Web Agents with Few-Shot Learning from Human Demonstrations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.13451) | web | Adapts multimodal web agents to unfamiliar websites using a small number of human demonstrations through prompting or meta-learning. |
| 2024‑11‑10 | [Is Your LLM Secretly a World Model of the Internet? Model-Based Planning for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.06559) | web | Introduces WebDreamer, which simulates candidate web actions with language-model world models and value estimates before acting. |
| 2024‑11‑07 | [Magentic-One: A Generalist Multi-Agent System for Solving Complex Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04468) | web, desktop | Introduces an orchestrated team of browsing, file, and coding agents with progress tracking and error recovery for general digital tasks. |
| 2024‑11‑06 | [From Novice to Expert: LLM Agent Policy Optimization via Step-wise Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.03817) | web | Introduces StepAgent, which compares agent and expert actions to supply intermediate rewards for policy learning, with implicit-reward and inverse-RL variants. Evaluation includes WebShop and Mind2Web alongside non-web tasks. |
| 2024‑11‑04 | [Attacking Vision-Language Computer Agents via Pop-ups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.02391) | desktop, web | Tests whether adversarial visual pop-ups divert computer agents from intended tasks in desktop and web benchmark environments. |
| 2024‑11‑04 | [WebRL: Training LLM Web Agents via Self-Evolving Online Curriculum Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.02337) | web | Trains web agents with an evolving task curriculum, outcome supervision, and reinforcement-learning updates intended to manage policy drift. |
| 2024‑10‑31 | [From Context to Action: Analysis of the Impact of State Representation and Context on the Generalization of Multi-Turn Web Navigation Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.23555) | web | Analyzes how interaction history and webpage representation affect multiturn web-agent generalization across unfamiliar websites, task categories, and geographic settings. |
| 2024‑10‑30 | [Evaluating Cultural and Social Awareness of LLM Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.23252) | web | Introduces CASA to assess web agents' responses to cultural and social norms in shopping and forum tasks, including misleading observations. |
| 2024‑10‑29 | [Auto-Intent: Automated Intent Discovery and Self-Exploration for Large Language Model Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22552) | web | Discovers compact intents from demonstrations and uses intent predictions to guide a language-model web agent's decisions and exploration. |
| 2024‑10‑28 | [AutoGLM: Autonomous Foundation Agents for GUIs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00820) | web, android | Presents AutoGLM agents for browser and phone control, separating planning from grounding and training through an evolving interaction curriculum. |
| 2024‑10‑25 | [EDGE: Enhanced Grounded GUI Understanding with Enriched Multi-Granularity Synthetic Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19461) | web, cross-platform | Synthesizes GUI training examples at multiple levels of detail from webpages and studies transfer of learned understanding to other interface environments. |
| 2024‑10‑25 | [OpenWebVoyager: Building Multimodal Web Agents via Iterative Real-World Exploration, Feedback and Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19609) | web | Develops multimodal browser agents through repeated cycles of real-web exploration, trajectory feedback, and policy optimization. |
| 2024‑10‑24 | [Ferret-UI 2: Mastering Universal User Interface Understanding Across Platforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18967) | ios, android, web, cross-platform | Extends Ferret-UI across device platforms with adaptive visual scaling and synthetic data for referring, grounding, and user-centered UI tasks. |
| 2024‑10‑24 | [Infogent: An Agent-Based Framework for Web Information Aggregation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19054) | web | Infogent separates navigation, extraction and aggregation for web information gathering, including browser interaction through webpage screenshots. |
| 2024‑10‑24 | [VideoWebArena: Evaluating Long Context Multimodal Agents with Video Understanding Web Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19100) | web | Evaluates whether web agents can retain procedural skills and factual information from long video tutorials and use them during tasks. |
| 2024‑10‑22 | [AdvAgent: Controllable Blackbox Red-teaming on Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17401) | web | Introduces a black-box web-agent red-teaming framework that learns adversarial prompts from agent feedback to study security weaknesses. |
| 2024‑10‑22 | [Large Language Models Empowered Personalized Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17236) | web | PersonalWAB benchmarks personalized web tasks, while PUMA retrieves relevant past web behavior to adapt agents' instruction handling and actions. |
| 2024‑10‑21 | [Beyond Browsing: API-Based Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16464) | web | Compares browsing-only, API-only, and hybrid web agents on WebArena, examining how access to application APIs changes task execution alongside browser interaction. |
| 2024‑10‑17 | [AgentOccam: A Simple Yet Strong Baseline for LLM-Based Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13825) | web | Studies how simplifying and aligning a web agent's observations and actions with language-model capabilities affects task completion. |
| 2024‑10‑17 | [Harnessing Webpage UIs for Text-Rich Visual Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13824) | web | Builds MultiUI by generating instructions from webpage accessibility trees and pairing them with screenshots for multimodal training. Evaluation examines web-interface understanding, including VisualWebBench and Mind2Web, and transfer to other text-rich visual tasks. |
| 2024‑10‑17 | [Web Agents with World Models: Learning and Leveraging Environment Dynamics in Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13232) | web | Uses a learned web world model to predict important state changes and compare candidate actions before executing them. |
| 2024‑10‑11 | [Refusal-Trained LLMs Are Easily Jailbroken As Browser Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13886) | web | Introduces BrowserART and studies whether chat-model refusal behavior transfers to browser agents carrying out potentially harmful instructions. |
| 2024‑10‑09 | [ST-WebAgentBench: A Benchmark for Evaluating Safety and Trustworthiness in Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06703) | web | Evaluates web-task completion under explicit enterprise policies, measuring policy-compliant completion and safety violations separately. |
| 2024‑10‑03 | [NNetNav: Unsupervised Learning of Browser Agents Through Environment Interaction in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02907) | web | Generates browser-agent demonstrations through autonomous exploration and retrospective task labeling, pruning trajectories that lack meaningful subtasks. |
| 2024‑10‑02 | [ExACT: Teaching AI Agents to Explore with Reflective-MCTS and Exploratory Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02052) | web | ExACT combines reflective tree search with learning from search experience to help web agents explore, assess states and backtrack on VisualWebArena. |
| 2024‑10‑01 | [Multimodal Auto Validation For Self-Refinement in Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00689) | web | Studies text and visual validation for web agents and uses the resulting validator to detect failures and guide workflow self-refinement. |
| 2024‑09‑24 | [Synatra: Turning Indirect Knowledge into Direct Demonstrations for Digital Agents at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15637) | web | Synatra converts indirect procedural knowledge, including human-oriented tutorials, into direct demonstrations for digital agents. It trains a 7B CodeLlama on 100,000 synthesized demonstrations and evaluates the resulting policy on Mind2Web, MiniWoB++, and WebArena. |
| 2024‑09‑23 | [Steward: Natural Language Web Automation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15441) | web | Introduces Steward, a natural-language browser automation system that repeatedly plans and executes website actions. The paper examines completion detection, state representation, responsiveness, and caching in real-site tasks. |
| 2024‑09‑21 | [StateAct: Enhancing LLM Base Agents via Self-prompting and State-tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02810) | web | Maintains explicit task goals and evolving state descriptions to improve base-agent decisions across interactive tasks, including web shopping. |
| 2024‑09‑18 | [The Impact of Element Ordering on LM Agent Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12089) | web, desktop | Studies how the ordering of UI elements in an agent observation affects web and desktop performance. A pixel-based element detector and dimensionality-reduction ordering are evaluated on OmniACT when document hierarchy is unavailable. |
| 2024‑09‑17 | [EIA: Environmental Injection Attack on Generalist Web Agents for Privacy Leakage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11295) | web | Evaluates privacy-directed webpage instruction injection against generalist web agents using PII-related interaction steps from Mind2Web, including attempts to expose specific user information or entire requests. |
| 2024‑09‑16 | [NaviQAte: Functionality-Guided Web Application Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.10741) | web | Frames web-application exploration as functionality-oriented question answering, generating navigation actions from multimodal context. |
| 2024‑09‑11 | [Agent Workflow Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.07429) | web | Agent Workflow Memory extracts reusable task routines and selectively supplies them to guide later web actions. It studies both offline workflow induction from training examples and online induction from test experience on Mind2Web and WebArena. |
| 2024‑09‑06 | [WebQuest: A Benchmark for Multimodal QA on Web Page Sequences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13711) | web | Introduces question answering over individual screens, multiple web pages, and navigation traces to test information synthesis across views. |
| 2024‑09‑03 | [From Grounding to Planning: Benchmarking Bottlenecks in Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01927) | web | Separates web-agent planning and grounding evaluations on Mind2Web to analyze which components limit task performance. |
| 2024‑08‑28 | [WebPilot: A Versatile and Autonomous Multi-Agent System for Web Task Execution with Strategic Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15978) | web | WebPilot separates global subtask planning from local Monte Carlo tree search during web execution. It continually refines the high-level plan and adapts local search to uncertain transitions and incomplete observations, evaluated on WebArena and MiniWoB++. |
| 2024‑08‑13 | [Agent Q: Advanced Reasoning and Learning for Autonomous AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07199) | web | Agent Q combines guided tree search, self-critique and preference-based fine-tuning to train web agents from successful and unsuccessful interactions. |
| 2024‑07‑29 | [MindSearch: Mimicking Human Minds Elicits Deep AI Searcher](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20183) | web | MindSearch uses a planner and searcher agents to split information requests into subquestions and gather answers across web pages. |
| 2024‑07‑23 | [OpenHands: An Open Platform for AI Software Developers as Generalist Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16741) | web | OpenHands is a platform for agents that write code, use a command line and browse the web, with sandboxed execution and support for agent evaluation. |
| 2024‑07‑22 | [AssistantBench: Can Web Agents Solve Realistic and Time-Consuming Tasks?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15711) | web | Introduces realistic open-web tasks and the SeePlanAct agent, analyzing the limitations of browsing and retrieval in time-consuming assistant work. |
| 2024‑07‑22 | [IDA: Breaking Barriers in No-code UI Automation Through Large Language Models and Human-Centric Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15673) | web | IDA helps business users create web UI automation through guided demonstrations, a semantic programming model, and a teaching-oriented interface. A prototype user study examines automation creation with enterprise applications by users without programming backgrounds. |
| 2024‑07‑17 | [Agent-E: From Autonomous Web Navigation to Foundational Design Principles in Agentic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13032) | web | Agent-E combines hierarchical web-task control with DOM distillation and observations of interface changes. WebVoyager experiments inform a broader discussion of primitive skills, observation reduction, and self-improvement in agent architecture. |
| 2024‑07‑15 | [Spider2-V: How Far Are Multimodal Agents From Automating Data Science and Engineering Workflows?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10956) | desktop, web | Benchmarks multimodal agents on data-science and engineering workflows involving enterprise applications, code, and GUI operations in real computer environments. |
| 2024‑07‑07 | [WorkArena++: Towards Compositional Planning and Reasoning-based Common Knowledge Work Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05291) | web | Defines compositional enterprise workflows to evaluate planning and reasoning, with generation of ground-truth interaction traces. |
| 2024‑07‑01 | [Tree Search for Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01476) | web | Applies best-first search to agent actions within interactive web environments, allowing exploration and multistep planning at inference time. WebArena and VisualWebArena experiments examine search behavior and test-time computation. |
| 2024‑06‑20 | [Identifying User Goals from UI Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14314) | android, web, cross-platform | Defines goal identification from observed UI trajectories and an evaluation method for comparing intent descriptions within the same interface context. | 2024‑06‑19 | [WONDERBREAD: A Benchmark for Evaluating Multimodal Foundation Models on Business Process Management Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13264) | web | Introduces WONDERBREAD, a benchmark pairing human WebArena workflow demonstrations with screen recordings, action logs, and written procedures. Six evaluation tasks cover documentation, knowledge transfer, and process improvement beyond end-to-end execution. |
| 2024‑06‑18 | [Dissecting Adversarial Robustness of Multimodal LM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12814) | web | Introduces adversarial tasks on VisualWebArena and an evaluation framework that tracks how manipulated information passes between agent components. The analysis examines vulnerabilities introduced by reflection and tree-search evaluators as well as direct action generation. |
| 2024‑06‑18 | [WebCanvas: Benchmarking Web Agents in Online Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12373) | web | Introduces WebCanvas and Mind2Web-Live, evaluating online task progress through meaningful intermediate states on changing websites. |
| 2024‑06‑14 | [VideoGUI: A Benchmark for GUI Automation from Instructional Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10227) | desktop, web | Evaluates GUI automation from instructional videos at separate levels of high-level planning, action narration, and primitive execution. |
| 2024‑06‑13 | [GuardAgent: Safeguard LLM Agents by a Guard Agent via Knowledge-Enabled Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09187) | web | Introduces GuardAgent, which translates safety requests into executable guardrail code using plans and retrieved demonstrations. Mind2Web-SC supplies a web-agent safety-policy evaluation alongside a separate healthcare access-control benchmark. |
| 2024‑06‑11 | [CAAP: Context-Aware Action Planning Prompting to Solve Computer Tasks with Front-End UI Only](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06947) | web | CAAP converts screenshots into textual observations and plans keyboard-and-mouse actions using a prompt that examines task context from multiple perspectives. The agent is evaluated on MiniWoB++ and WebShop using only front-end visual observations. |
| 2024‑06‑01 | [WebSuite: Systematically Evaluating Why Web Agents Fail](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01623) | web | WebSuite organizes browser operations into an action taxonomy and tests both isolated controls and complete workflows. Its diagnostic tasks attribute failures to specific actions, enabling comparison of text-based and multimodal web agents. |
| 2024‑05‑30 | [Large Language Models Can Self-Improve At Web Agent Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20309) | web | Examines self-improvement by fine-tuning web agents on three mixtures of model-generated training data. WebArena experiments and trajectory-level evaluation measures study task performance, robustness, and behavior beyond aggregate success rates. |
| 2024‑05‑07 | [Unveiling Disparities in Web Task Handling Between Human and Web Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04497) | web | Compares human think-aloud web-task behavior with web-agent planning, action, and reflection. The analysis highlights differences in updating plans, handling ambiguity, and investigating failures. |
| 2024‑05‑01 | [Navigating WebAI: Training Agents to Complete Web Tasks with Large Language Models and Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00516) | web | Combines supervised and reinforcement learning for MiniWoB web tasks and investigates whether agents understand HTML structure or memorize target elements. |
| 2024‑04‑19 | [AutoScraper: A Progressive Understanding Web Agent for Web Scraper Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.12753) | web | Introduces AutoScraper, a two-stage framework that uses HTML structure and similarities across pages to generate scrapers for data collection from websites. |
| 2024‑04‑16 | [Grounded Language Agent for Product Search via Intelligent Web Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10887) | web | Introduces GLAINTEL, a Flan-T5-based product-search web agent studied under unsupervised learning, demonstration-based training, and unsupervised domain adaptation. It compares ways to combine demonstrations with reinforcement learning. |
| 2024‑04‑15 | [MMInA: Benchmarking Multihop Multimodal Internet Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09992) | web | Evaluates multimodal agents on compositional tasks across changing websites and studies replaying prior trajectories as memory support. |
| 2024‑04‑09 | [Autonomous Evaluation and Refinement of Digital Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.06474) | web | Compares automatic trajectory evaluators for web navigation and device control against oracle judgments. The resulting feedback is used for agent fine-tuning and inference-time guidance, including experiments on WebArena. |
| 2024‑04‑09 | [GUIDE: Graphical User Interface Data for Execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16048) | web | Introduces annotated website task trajectories with screenshots, prior actions, reasoning, and grounded next actions for process automation. |
| 2024‑04‑09 | [VisualWebBench: How Far Have Multimodal LLMs Evolved in Web Page Understanding and Grounding?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05955) | web | Separately evaluates web-page reading, understanding, and element grounding across tasks drawn from real websites. |
| 2024‑04‑08 | [WILBUR: Adaptive In-Context Learning for Robust and Accurate Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05902) | web | WILBUR learns to rank prior task demonstrations for a web agent’s prompt and synthesizes instructions from past runs. A generated task curriculum and a backtracking mechanism support adaptation and recovery, evaluated on WebVoyager. |
| 2024‑04‑04 | [AutoWebGLM: A Large Language Model-based Web Navigating Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03648) | web | Builds a web agent through simplified HTML observations, human-and-AI browsing data, curriculum training, and reinforcement-learning refinement. |
| 2024‑03‑18 | [Tur\[k\]ingBench: A Challenge Benchmark for Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11905) | web | Evaluates web agents on multimodal annotation tasks adapted from HTML interfaces originally built for crowdsourcing workers. |
| 2024‑03‑13 | [AutoGuide: Automated Generation and Selection of Context-Aware Guidelines for Large Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08978) | web | Introduces AutoGuide, which generates conditional, context-aware guidelines from past agent experience to support decisions in sequential tasks, including web navigation. |
| 2024‑03‑12 | [WorkArena: How Capable Are Web Agents at Solving Common Knowledge Work Tasks?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07718) | web | Introduces enterprise web tasks on ServiceNow and BrowserGym interfaces for evaluating browser-based agents. |
| 2024‑02‑27 | [OmniACT: A Dataset and Benchmark for Enabling Multimodal Generalist Autonomous Agents for Desktop and Web](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17553) | desktop, web | Introduces a dataset and benchmark for generating executable task scripts from screenshots and visually grounded desktop or web instructions. |
| 2024‑02‑26 | [WIPI: A New Web Threat for LLM-Driven Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16965) | web | Studies WIPI, a black-box indirect-instruction threat in which webpage content redirects LLM-driven web agents. Evaluation spans plugin-based agents, Web GPTs, and open-source web-agent implementations. |
| 2024‑02‑23 | [On the Multi-turn Instruction Following for Conversational Web Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15057) | web | MT-Mind2Web evaluates web navigation through sequences of conversational user instructions. Self-MAP combines memory-augmented planning with reflection to preserve context and dependencies across those interaction turns. |
| 2024‑02‑12 | [OS-Copilot: Towards Generalist Computer Agents with Self-Improvement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07456) | desktop, web | Provides an operating-system agent framework connecting applications, files, terminals, and web tools, with accumulated skills for subsequent tasks. |
| 2024‑02‑08 | [WebLINX: Real-World Website Navigation with Multi-Turn Dialogue](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05930) | web | Collects expert demonstrations of conversational web navigation and studies retrieval-based element selection for processing large pages. |
| 2024‑02‑07 | [UEyes: An Eye-Tracking Dataset across User Interface Types](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05202) | web, desktop | Introduces UEyes, an eye-tracking dataset of webpage, desktop, mobile, and poster interfaces, and analyzes how people view these different UI types. |
| 2024‑02‑06 | [Dual-View Visual Contextualization for Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04476) | web | Uses screenshot geometry and visual content to enrich HTML element representations for web navigation. Nearby elements contribute textual and visual context, with evaluation on cross-task, cross-website, and cross-domain Mind2Web splits. |
| 2024‑01‑25 | [WebVoyager: Building an End-to-End Web Agent with Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13919) | web | Combines multimodal web interaction with a real-website task collection and an automated evaluation protocol. |
| 2024‑01‑24 | [AgentBoard: An Analytical Evaluation Board of Multi-turn LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13178) | web | Introduces AgentBoard, a multiturn agent evaluation framework with intermediate progress metrics and analysis tools. Its broader task suite includes WebShop and WebArena interactions with stepwise progress measurements. |
| 2024‑01‑24 | [VisualWebArena: Evaluating Multimodal Agents on Realistic Visual Web Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13649) | web | Introduces visually grounded website tasks for evaluating multimodal agents that must interpret both images and text. |
| 2024‑01‑03 | [GPT-4V(ision) is a Generalist Web Agent, if Grounded](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01614) | web | Introduces SeeAct and live-website evaluation, examining how visual planning and element grounding affect web-agent task completion. |
| 2023‑12‑25 | [WebVLN: Vision-and-Language Navigation on Websites](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.15820) | web | WebVLN trains website navigation from question-based instructions using both rendered visual information and underlying HTML. It introduces WebVLN-v1 and a website-aware navigation network to study this digital variant of vision-and-language navigation. |
| 2023‑12‑14 | [CogAgent: A Visual Language Model for GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.08914) | desktop, android, web | Introduces a visual-language model with low- and high-resolution image encoders for screenshot understanding and GUI navigation. |
| 2023‑11‑30 | [Exposing Limitations of Language Model Agents in Sequential-Task Compositions on the Web](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.18751) | web | CompWoB adds 50 composed web-automation tasks to study generalization from simpler interactions. The paper compares prompted and fine-tuned agents, balances task training data, and probes sensitivity to changes in instruction order. |
| 2023‑11‑21 | [GAIA: a benchmark for General AI Assistants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.12983) | web | Introduces questions that test assistant reasoning, multimodal understanding, browsing, and tool use beyond screen-action execution alone. |
| 2023‑10‑24 | [WebWISE: Web Interface Control and Sequential Exploration with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.16042) | web | WebWISE filters DOM elements into observations and generates short programs for click, scroll, and text-entry steps. It evaluates in-context learning from either one supplied example or an example collected during a successful zero-shot MiniWoB++ trial. |
| 2023‑10‑20 | [AllTogether: Investigating the Efficacy of Spliced Prompt for Web Navigation using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.18331) | web | AllTogether standardizes prompts for HTML-based web navigation and compares prompting with instruction fine-tuning. Its analysis examines how HTML context, interaction history, and current environmental feedback affect task execution. |
| 2023‑10‑16 | [OpenAgents: An Open Platform for Language Agents in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.10634) | web | OpenAgents hosts three language agents, for data analysis, API plugins and autonomous web browsing, behind a web interface for non-expert users, with local deployment for developers. |
| 2023‑10‑12 | [A Zero-Shot Language Agent for Computer Control with Structured Reflection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.08740) | web | Studies a zero-shot computer-control agent that plans executable actions and organizes its own reflections after mistakes. MiniWoB++ experiments examine learning to complete tasks without supplied expert trajectories. |
| 2023‑10‑08 | [UReader: Universal OCR-free Visually-situated Language Understanding with Multimodal Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.05126) | web | Introduces UReader, which combines unified instruction tuning, auxiliary text-reading tasks and shape-adaptive image crops for OCR-free visual language understanding. Its evaluation includes webpage screenshots alongside documents, tables, charts and natural images. |
| 2023‑10‑05 | [SteP: Stacked LLM Policies for Web Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.03720) | web | SteP dynamically composes specialized web-action policies through a stack that records the active chain of policy calls. It studies this control structure across WebArena, MiniWoB++, and CRM workflows. |
| 2023‑09‑15 | [LASER: LLM Agent with State-Space Exploration for Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.08172) | web | LASER represents web navigation as exploration over a defined set of states, allowing an agent to revisit earlier states after mistakes. The study evaluates this backtracking approach on WebShop and Amazon tasks. |
| 2023‑07‑25 | [WebArena: A Realistic Web Environment for Building Autonomous Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.13854) | web | Introduces reproducible web environments with task-completion checks for evaluating autonomous web agents. |
| 2023‑07‑24 | [A Real-World WebAgent with Planning, Long Context Understanding, and Program Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.12856) | web | Combines task decomposition, HTML summarization, and generated Python programs to carry out instructions on websites. |
| 2023‑06‑13 | [Synapse: Trajectory-as-Exemplar Prompting with Memory for Computer Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.07863) | web | Synapse filters task-irrelevant page state and places complete abstracted state-action trajectories into the prompt. An embedding-based exemplar memory retrieves relevant demonstrations for new tasks, evaluated on MiniWoB++ and Mind2Web. |
| 2023‑06‑09 | [Mind2Web: Towards a Generalist Agent for the Web](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.06070) | web | Introduces a web-task dataset spanning diverse websites and evaluates generalization to unseen sites and domains. |
| 2023‑05‑31 | [From Pixels to UI Actions: Learning to Follow Instructions via Graphical User Interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.00245) | web | Studies instruction-following agents that observe screenshots and act through a generic mouse-and-keyboard action space, evaluating pixel-based pretraining for GUI tasks on MiniWoB++. |
| 2023‑05‑26 | [AdaPlanner: Adaptive Planning from Feedback with Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.16653) | web | Introduces AdaPlanner, which revises code-structured plans in response to environmental feedback and reuses successful plans as exemplars. Experiments include MiniWoB++ web tasks alongside ALFWorld. |
| 2023‑05‑23 | [Hierarchical Prompting Assists Large Language Model on Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.14257) | web | Separates web navigation prompting into a SUMMARIZER that extracts action-relevant observations and an ACTOR that chooses the next action from the condensed context. |
| 2023‑05‑19 | [Multimodal Web Navigation with Instruction-Finetuned Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.11854) | web | Trains WebGUM offline from demonstrations by jointly tuning an instruction-following language model and a vision encoder over webpage screenshots and HTML. The agent predicts browser actions and is evaluated on MiniWoB, WebShop, and Mind2Web. |
| 2023‑03‑30 | [Language Models can Solve Computer Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.17491) | web | Introduces recursive criticism and improvement prompting for language-model agents performing computer tasks, evaluated on MiniWoB++ with a small number of demonstrations per task. |
| 2023‑01‑30 | [WebUI: A Dataset for Enhancing Visual UI Understanding with Web Semantics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2301.13280) | web | Collects rendered webpages and structural metadata for visual UI modeling, studying how web semantics transfer to interface-understanding tasks. |
| 2022‑10‑08 | [Understanding HTML with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.03945) | web | Studies language models for HTML element classification, page description and autonomous web navigation. Fine-tuned models are evaluated on MiniWoB, and a distilled, automatically labeled CommonCrawl HTML corpus supports the training approach. |
| 2022‑10‑07 | [Pix2Struct: Screenshot Parsing as Pretraining for Visual Language Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.03347) | web | Pix2Struct pretrains an image-to-text model by reconstructing simplified HTML from masked webpage screenshots. Its variable-resolution visual representation is evaluated across several domains, including user-interface understanding. |
| 2022‑07‑04 | [WebShop: Towards Scalable Real-World Web Interaction with Grounded Language Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.01206) | web | Provides a simulated shopping environment where language agents search, configure, and select products from natural-language requirements. |
| 2022‑02‑16 | [A data-driven approach for learning to control computers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.08137) | web | Combines reinforcement learning with behavioral priors from human interactions to learn computer control through keyboard and mouse actions. |
| 2021‑12‑17 | [WebGPT: Browser-assisted question-answering with human feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.09332) | web | Trains a question-answering model to search and navigate a text-based web browser, collect references and improve long-form answers using demonstrations and human feedback. |
| 2021‑03‑30 | [Grounding Open-Domain Instructions to Automate Web Support Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2103.16057) | web | Introduces RUSS, which translates support instructions into a domain-specific language and grounds them in actionable web elements. |
| 2021‑01‑23 | [WebSRC: A Dataset for Web-Based Structural Reading Comprehension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2101.09465) | web | Introduces question answering over webpage text and structure, with aligned HTML, screenshots, and metadata for structural reading comprehension. |
| 2019‑02‑19 | [DOM-Q-NET: Grounded RL on Structured Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/1902.07257) | web | Introduces DOM-Q-NET, an RL architecture for web navigation that represents HTML as a tree and uses separate Q-function networks for clicking elements and typing text. |
| 2018‑12‑21 | [Learning to Navigate the Web](https://arxiv.org/abs/1812.09195) | web | Introduces guided RL for QWeb, using a curriculum of smaller instructions and generated tasks to train an agent that follows instructions and fills forms on web pages. |
| 2018‑08‑28 | [Mapping Natural Language Commands to Web Elements](https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.09132) | web | Defines language grounding on webpages as choosing the UI element targeted by a natural-language command. A collection of more than 50,000 commands and three baseline models study functional references, relationships between elements, and visual position cues. |
| 2018‑02‑24 | [Reinforcement Learning on Web Interfaces Using Workflow-Guided Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.08802) | web | Uses workflows extracted from demonstrations to constrain reinforcement-learning exploration for interactive web tasks. |
| 2018 | [Rousillon: Scraping Distributed Hierarchical Web Data](https://doi.org/10.1145/3242587.3242661) | web | Introduces Rousillon, which generalizes a demonstration of collecting one hierarchical data row into a browser-automation program that traverses related pages. The paper develops relation-selection and generalization methods and evaluates users building such scripts. |
| 2017‑07‑17 | [World of Bits: An Open-Domain Platform for Web-Based Agents](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v70/shi17a.html) | web | Introduces World of Bits, a web-task platform combining human demonstrations, keyboard and mouse actions, and cached HTTP traffic for repeatable task environments. It evaluates agents trained through behavioral cloning and reinforcement learning. |
| 2016‑11 | [Ringer: Web Automation by Demonstration](https://doi.org/10.1145/2983990.2984020) | web | Introduces Ringer, which converts demonstrated webpage interactions into scripts combining actions, target selectors, and waiting conditions. It evaluates replay robustness on tasks recorded from real websites. |
| 2016‑02‑06 | [End-to-End Goal-Driven Web Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/1602.02261) | web | Introduces WebNav for converting websites into goal-directed hyperlink navigation tasks and WikiNav for evaluating language-conditioned navigation and question answering on Wikipedia. |
| 2007 | [Koala: Capture, Share, Automate, Personalize Business Processes on the Web](https://doi.org/10.1145/1240624.1240767) | web | Introduces Koala, a collaborative web automation system that records browser interactions as editable pseudo-natural-language scripts. Scripts are shared through a wiki and instantiated with personal or corporate data for reuse by other users. |
| 2007 | [PLOW: A Collaborative Task Learning Agent](https://aaai.org/Papers/AAAI/2007/AAAI07-240.pdf) | web | Describes PLOW, which learns executable web-task models from a session combining demonstrations, natural-language explanations, and dialogue. Browser instrumentation connects user actions with DOM structure to support task generalization. |
